| D13 | Wellbeing radar (opt-in, privacy-first) | **Done** | **Migration 0125** (`wellbeing_settings`: opted in, counsellor consent, time zone, last nudge; `wellbeing_checkins`: one per local day: mood 1–5, tags, a private note; `wellbeing_alerts`: ASKED or PATTERN, OPEN → SEEN → CLOSED). **Off until the student turns it on** (Help & support → **Wellbeing**, `/student/wellbeing`), after an explanation of what it looks at, what it never does (no AI on personal data; teachers never see it) and who hears about it. **Rules, no AI** (`src/lib/wellbeing.ts`, 5 tests): late nights (handed-in work or AI help between midnight and 5 a.m. in the student's own time zone, ≥ 3 nights in 7), “I'm lost” in in-person classes (D7, ≥ 3 in 14 days), missed deadlines (assignments and quizzes due in the last 14 days and never handed in, ≥ 2), a heavy few days (≥ 4 due in the next 3), hard days (mood ≤ 2 on ≥ 3 of 7, or a very hard day lately) → private nudges with a link (planner, assignments, tutor, Talk to someone); a good week gets a kind word. **Counsellors** are staff given the new **`wellbeing.counsel`** permission (custom roles, B15.6; Office → **Wellbeing requests**, `/admin/wellbeing`; admins reach it from Safety); if nobody has it, requests go to the school admins (the student is told which). They hear only when the student taps **Talk to someone** (one open request at a time, an optional message), or, only if the student ticked the box, when check-ins show a really hard stretch (mood ≤ 2 on ≥ 4 of 7 days, or very hard on 2 of the last 3), at most once a fortnight, never with the notes. **Mark as seen** tells the student someone will reach out. The daily cron sends a private in-app nudge (no email) at most every 3 days to up to 25 students per run, and deletes check-ins older than 90 days. **Turn off and delete** removes the settings and check-ins and withdraws unseen requests. A crisis note is always on the page. APIs: `/api/wellbeing` (GET, POST join / settings / check-in / ask / leave), `/api/wellbeing/requests` (GET, POST seen / close). 6 server tests, 18 API checks, Playwright + axe at 390 px light and dark. **Not done:** a counsellor's reply inside UniVerse (they reach out their usual way); local crisis numbers per school. |

| D17 | Offline classroom relay (experimental) | **Done (moving QR codes; the owner's choice)** | No migration. Research first: phone-to-phone WebRTC over a classroom hotspot isn't reliable enough (QR handshakes per student with no internet, client isolation on many networks, `.local` addresses, no Web Bluetooth sending), so the owner chose **moving QR codes** for short items. New library **jsQR 1.4.0** (Apache-2.0; reads codes on phones without a built-in reader; only in the scanner's own chunk). **`src/lib/qr.ts`**: versions up to 15 (was 10) and QR's compact **alphanumeric mode** when the text allows (byte mode otherwise, so links are unchanged). **`src/lib/qr-relay.ts`** (6 tests, including encoder → jsQR read-back at all three sizes): an item (`NOTE` / `ANNOUNCEMENT` / `QUIZ` with questions and options only) as `[format][flags][key id][Ed25519 signature][deflated JSON]`, Base45 (RFC 9285), cut into frames `UV1:<loop id>:<n>/<total>:<data>` of 180 / 330 / 570 characters (QR versions 7 / 11 / 15); `FrameCollector` takes frames in any order, ignores other codes and a stray frame of another loop, switches when the teacher shows something else; `checkRelay` checks the signature with WebCrypto (`ok` / `bad` / `unsigned` / `unchecked`). **`src/server/qr-relay.ts`**: `GET /api/classroom/relay?courseId=` (the course's announcements and quizzes, each with why it can't be sent: pictures, exam mode, draft, closed, too long, over 40 questions) and `POST` (a note typed now, an announcement or a quiz; built from the database, signed with the platform key `CREDENTIAL_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` via new `CredentialSigner.signText`; unsigned when there's no key); the course's teacher or an admin only. Sample stub. **Teacher:** Live class → **Send to phones with no internet** (`components/live/QrRelayPanel.tsx`): Note / Announcement / Quiz → **Show as codes**: full screen, the loop at Slow / Normal / Fast (2 / 4 / 6 codes a second), Small / Medium / Large codes, Pause and Next code, "Signed"; prepared items stay **ready on this computer** (up to 8, browser storage) and show with no connection. **Students:** Offline courses → **From the teacher’s screen** → **Scan from the teacher’s screen** (`components/offline/QrRelayScan.tsx`, camera in `QrRelayCamera.tsx`: the phone's BarcodeDetector where there is one, else jsQR): progress per code, then the item is checked against the school's key (kept from `/api/passport/jwks` while online; an item that fails is never saved, one that couldn't be checked yet is checked next time online) and kept on the phone (Cache Storage, 30 items): notes and announcements to read, quizzes to take offline with the existing offline quiz (answers go to the outbox and are marked on the server when online). Opening Offline courses online fetches the scanner ahead so it works offline. Live class (student) links to it (`?scan=1`); search and help entries. **Also fixed:** the offline bar's white text on orange/green (2.2:1) is dark now; the Offline page's own offline note is darker; the phone header's "Home" link is named "UniVerse home" (it shows "UniVerse"). **Tested locally:** API script (24 checks: who may list and prepare, every refusal reason, note / announcement / quiz prepared and signed, no answers in a quiz, the signature against the public key, the whole trip for each kind, a changed item fails, the quiz's answers marked on the server); Playwright end to end (24 checks: the teacher shows a note and a quiz, the codes are read off the screen into a fake camera video; a 390 px student phone **offline** reads them in under 2 s, "Checked: sent by Dr. Jane Smith", keeps it, takes the quiz offline, and back online it's sent and marked 3 of 3; a 5,500-character note in 7 small codes read in 2.5 s); axe clean light and dark (teacher page and code screen, student page, scanner, note, quiz). **Not tried:** real phones and a real projector (the owner's school); iPhone (Safari) reading; the BarcodeDetector path (Chrome on Linux has none). |

| FREE.1 | Live updates reach everyone in big groups (Workers Free) | **Done** | No migration. Telling a person's live hub is one subrequest and Workers Free allows 50 per request, so a message in a 100-member group reached only about the first 50 live. **`src/lib/fanout.ts`** (5 tests, simulating the 50 rule for 100 and 5,000 people): a sender tells at most 40 hubs itself; for more it hands each hub a slice ("relay": that hub tells its own tabs, then the rest of its slice, with its own allowance), so any number of people is reached in a few hops, still one hub request per person. `RealtimeHub` answers `/relay` (`cloudflare/worker.ts`); `deliver()` in `src/server/realtime.ts` uses it. The 40-person caps on class, group, module, call and companion updates are gone; typing indicators are skipped above the plan's live-push size (40 free, 500 paid). **Tested locally (built Worker):** 101 people with UniVerse open (one live connection each, test accounts), a message in their 101-member group reached 101 / 101 in 0.28 s, nobody twice. |
| FREE.2 | Warning before the free plan's daily allowances run out | **Done** | **Migration 0129** (`usage_guard.daily`, `dailyAlerted`). Workers Free has daily allowances (100,000 requests, 100,000 live-hub requests, 13,000 GB-s, 5 M rows read, 100,000 rows written; reset 00:00 UTC = 5:30 AM India) and nothing warned before them. The spending guard (`cloudflare/usage-guard.ts`, every 15 min) now reads today's use too and warns the owners in the app and by push (no email) at 70 % and 90 %, once each a day (`/api/cron/usage-alert`). Also fixed: on the free plan the guard could pause the app from the $5 plan's monthly figures; now only files (R2) can pause it there. Console → Server shows "Cloudflare free plan · today"; the owner's morning summary and insights use the same figures (`src/server/plan-usage.ts`). 4 tests (`src/server/usage-guard.test.ts`). Needs `CF_ACCOUNT_ID` / `CF_USAGE_TOKEN` (DEPLOY-CLOUDFLARE.md); `WORKERS_PAID=true` after upgrading. |
| Q1 | Pages with an id no longer start Next.js (no more "Error 1101" on them) | **Done** | No migration. The 24 client pages (+ `/receipt/[id]`, now a client page reading the new `GET /api/receipts/[id]`, public by id, completed payments only, sample stub) are built once with every parameter "open" (`generateStaticParams` in each `page.tsx`; the old code is `View.tsx`, which reads the values from the address with **`usePathParams`** (`src/hooks/usePathParams.ts`, `src/lib/path-params.ts`; nothing id-specific renders before hydration, so no #418) and shows `AddressLoading` meanwhile). **`scripts/gen-api-routes.mjs`** now also writes **`cloudflare/page-routes.gen.ts`** (every page in Next.js's match order, with the built page for each; `--check` and `src/lib/page-routes.test.ts` keep it current: run it after adding a page). **`cloudflare/page-match.ts`** picks the page like Next.js and the Worker answers `/docs/<id>` with `/docs/open` (from the cache, Next.js not started); `/join/<code>` and `/student/courses/<id>` are 302s from the Worker, keeping the query. **Trap found and fixed:** Next.js's router knows such a page as `/docs/open` while the address says `/docs/<id>`; a link's prefetch to or from one filed the data under one name and looked under the other, and asked again (300 requests in 5 s on the assignments list, which tripped the tab's request brake). The Worker answers those prefetches with 204 (to a built page, or with `Next-Url` naming one); a click loads the page as usual. Also: the whiteboard's menu button and the code editor have accessible names; contrast on the scholarship check page; the receipt page closes its 404 answer. **Tested locally (built Worker, fresh start each time):** all 28 addresses answer in 0.03–0.10 s, and a page that needs Next.js right after still takes 1.8–3.2 s; Playwright at 390 px (light and dark, axe) and 1280 px: every page opens directly with its own data, no page errors; boards list → board and assignments list → assignment → back, in-app; two profiles in a row show their own data; 10 s on each page: no request loops (0–1 requests). **Note for new pages with an id:** copy one of these (page.tsx + View.tsx), then run `node scripts/gen-api-routes.mjs`. |

no password, no code). **Admins:** the second step offers the passkey first and sends no email unless asked ("Email me a code instead"). **Rate limit:** `AUTH_RATE_LIMITER` 10 a minute for sign-in steps (demo sign-in per address; 11th sign-in attempt in a minute → 429; CSP report stored once without the query; recording link for the class, 403 for others, 401 signed out; passkey removed → no sign-in) and Playwright at 390 px with Chromium's virtual authenticator (add a passkey, axe light and dark on Settings and the sign-in page, sign out, sign in with "Passkey", "This device" in the list). 9 unit tests. **Not done:** the admin second-step screen with a passkey was tested through the API only (a real Firebase sign-in is needed for the page); The console tab itself wasn't opened: being the owner needs a real Google sign-in, which a local test can't do. **Fixed while testing:** the AI chart's weekday labels were a day early west of UTC (a date-only string is read as UTC midnight), and the error screen's "Reference" line was too faint for WCAG contrast. |
| Q4 | A4 Performance budget (finish) | **Done** (LCP/INP p75 for real people: read them in owner console → Analytics → Speed after the deploy) | **No migration.** **Three sizes of every uploaded photo:** the browser makes them before uploading (`src/lib/photo-sizes.ts`: longest side 320, 960 and 1920 px, or fewer when the photo is smaller; WebP, else JPEG, PNG kept for transparency; redrawing drops camera data) and sends them in one request, `POST /api/upload/sized` (multipart `w<width>` parts, 2–4 sizes, same picture type, 4 MB together; covered by the Uploads switch, the costly-calls limit and sample mode). Nothing new in the database: the photo's address says which sizes there are (`src/lib/image-sizes.ts`: key `<24 random>-s320-960-1920`, each smaller size is `<random>-<width>`), so old photos and other files are untouched. `uploadChatFile` tries this first for every photo (chat, albums, call chat, status, stickers, avatars, logos, course board, doc pictures), falling back to the single upload; group posts now use it too. `photoSrcSet(url, shownWidth)` adds `srcset`/`sizes` on avatars (`Avatar` and 7 other avatar spots), chat photos, albums, stickers, the media gallery, inline attachments, group post photos, statuses and campus logos. `sizes` for photos without a fixed box is the widest they can be shown (a bubble: `(max-width: 640px) 82vw, 544px`), because it sets the picture's natural size. `deleteFile` removes the smaller sizes with the photo. `authedFetch` no longer labels a form as JSON. **Measured on the built Worker:** a 4000×3000 photo sent in a chat went up in one request as 320/960/1920 px (22/70/140 KB); a phone with a 3× screen loaded the 960 version, a 1× phone the 320 one, a 1280 px laptop (2×) the 1920 one, each shown at the same size as before; an avatar loaded the 22 KB version instead of the full photo; bad forms (one size, mixed types, SVG, repeated widths, 5 MB) were refused. **N+1 review:** `src/server/services/*` had no loops of queries; fixed elsewhere: the peer tutor list (2 queries per tutor → 2 in all, the next free slot with one window-function query), opening a doc (one query per page above it → one recursive query) and a folder in Files (the same). **Peer tutoring fixes found on the way:** "verified by Learning DNA" was given to every class a student was in, without looking at anything; it now comes from the student's real concept mastery (`conceptMasteryOf` in `src/server/learning-dna.ts`, up to 4 classes with concepts), and a profile save keeps "verified" only where that check agrees. The profile request is cleaned (lengths capped, 20 subjects) and the school is the person's own, never the request's; the public tutor list no longer includes email addresses; a bad `page` no longer fails. **Cache-Control:** the public answers that are the same for everyone already had it (branding, manifest, trust, OpenAPI, LTI keys, passport issuer, sign-in lookup, league and impact-room pages); the public passport and impact reports stay `no-store` so hiding them works at once. **Also fixed (security, own commit):** the OneRoster roster reads answered anyone (see the handoff). |
| Q5.1 | Copy a course to a new term | **Done** | **No migration.** Course board (teacher) → **Copy to a new term**: a new code and name, then dates are cleared or moved by whole weeks (same weekday). Copies modules (unlock rules and release dates), items, materials (the same files: deleting a material never deletes its file), quizzes (as drafts) and their questions, assignments and rubrics, gradebook categories, the question bank, Learning DNA concepts (parents first) and tags, and oral checks; never students, work, grades, attendance, announcements or chats; a module's live class is left for the teacher to date again. **How:** `src/server/course-copy.ts`, `POST /api/courses/[id]/copy` (makes the draft course) then `POST …/copy/step` per stage, 90 rows a step, because Workers Free allows 50 queries per request. Each copied row's id comes from the new course's id and the original's (`copiedId`), so steps can point at each other's rows, a repeated step skips what's there (the dialog's **Try again** carries on where it stopped) and nothing is stored between steps. `COLUMNS` per table keeps each insert under D1's 100 values (checked against the schema by `course-copy.test.ts`). **Tested on the built Worker** with a course of 2 modules (6 items of every kind), a 95-question quiz, an assignment with a rubric, a category, bank question, 3 concept tags and an oral check: 14 requests; every item, unlock rule, tag and category points at the copy's own rows; dates moved 52 weeks; a step run twice copied 0; a student is refused (403), copying into itself too (400), a taken code gets 409. |

Students can hide their name from classmates (the teacher always sees it; teachers can't post anonymously). Filters All / Unanswered / Mine and search. **Teacher's AI draft:** "Draft an answer with AI" fills the reply box from the course's own materials with [n] citations (`coursePassages`, one AI request via `spendAi`, on the AI receipt as "Discussion answer drafts", covered by the AI switch and the costly-calls limit); nothing is posted by itself. In-app notices only (new question → teacher; reply → asker; marked as the answer / endorsed → that reply's author), each opening the thread on the person's own board; live refresh for the people in the thread. Personal data export picks the tables up by itself. **Code:** `src/server/course-discussion.ts` (2 tests), `/api/courses/[id]/discussion` (+ `/[threadId]`, `/[threadId]/suggest`), `components/dashboard/CourseDiscussion.tsx`, sample-mode thread. **Tested on the built Worker:** 20 API checks (anonymous for classmates but not the teacher, own-vote refused, vote toggles, accept only by asker/teacher, endorse/lock only by the teacher, locked threads refuse replies, others can't edit, deleting the accepted reply makes it unanswered again, filters and search, the AI draft is teacher-only and says when there are no materials, another course's address 404) and the notices. |

`assertManagesCourse` counts co-teachers, the new `assertGradesCourse` assistants too (attendance); the gradebook, quiz access (`manageableQuiz`, quizzes service), the tutor's teacher view, consent forms, early warning, support plans, marketplace import, office notes, impact verification and parent messages count co-teachers; | Q5.8 | Import and export as Common Cartridge | **Done** (tried on the built Worker: a Canvas-style test cartridge imported in the browser and the course exported and read back; 390 and 1280 px, light and dark, axe clean). Google Classroom: **[owner]**, see the note | **No migration.** Course board → Modules → **Import or export** (teachers and co-teachers). **Export**: the modules as an IMS Common Cartridge 1.1 (`.imscc`, which Canvas, Moodle, Blackboard, Brightspace and Schoology import), made in the browser (`src/lib/cc-export.ts`, zipped with fflate): pages as HTML (rich pages with their blocks written out: a quick check as the question, choices and answer, flashcards as a list, videos as links with their chapters, maths as LaTeX), links and videos as web links, course files inside the cartridge (up to 25 MB each, 300 MB in all; bigger ones as links), quizzes as QTI 1.2 multiple choice with the right answer and points, assignments as pages with their instructions, points and due date. Live classes and students' work stay here. One read for the structure (`GET /api/courses/[id]/cartridge`), then rich pages one by one. **Import**: choose a `.imscc` (or zip, up to 300 MB): it's read in the browser (`src/lib/cc-import.ts` with a small XML reader, `src/lib/xml-lite.ts`: no external entities) and shown first: the cartridge's title, how many pages, links, videos, files and quizzes, its modules, and what won't come in and why (external LTI tools, essay and other non-multiple-choice questions, missing files, links that aren't web addresses). The folders become modules (folders inside folders become their own modules after them), **hidden from students** until the teacher shows them. Pages go into the page editor's format (pictures from the cartridge uploaded, links into the old system removed, scripts dropped, the repeated title at the top removed); files are uploaded and become course materials; YouTube links become videos; discussion topics and assignments come in as pages with their text; QTI multiple-choice and true/false questions become **draft quizzes**. Sent a few items at a time (`POST /api/courses/[id]/import`, at most 20 items, 24 database writes and 250 kB a request: `importBatches`), so a big course stays within Workers Free limits; it can be stopped, and what was added stays. The server checks everything again (pages like the editor's, links, files only if uploaded here, quiz questions with distinct choices and a right answer). Sample mode: export works, import says it isn't saved. 18 unit tests (`cc-export.test.ts`, `cc-import.test.ts`: XML, a Canvas-style cartridge, QTI, paths, pictures, reading back our own export). **Google Classroom** has no cartridge export and its Takeout format isn't documented: importing it needs a sample Takeout export from the owner, or the Classroom API (an app registration, **[owner]**). |

The rules are pure functions (`src/lib/assignment-policy.ts`, 7 tests). Teacher's assignment page → **Rules and extensions**: late penalty (% per started day after the grace minutes, up to a maximum), how many hand-ins before grading (empty: any), saved with the assignment and copied with a course to a new term; extensions (the teaching team: student, new date, extra hand-ins, a note; they're told in the app, and a closed assignment stays open for them until then). A hand-in is measured against the student's own due date (their extension, or the due date plus their extra days); its late days and % are saved then, and taken off when it's graded (the grader shows "→ 6.4 after −20% late" and a box to waive it); a third hand-in over a limit of 2 is refused. **Let them hand in again** on a returned answer (REOPENED: the last grade shows until the new one is graded; it counts as returned in the to-grade counts). Students see their own due date ("your own date"), the rules in a sentence and hand-ins left, and on the grade "8/10 less 20% for handing it in 2 days late" or that it was waived. **Extra time** (Class list → the clock by a student: teacher and co-teachers, audited): extra days on every assignment, and extra % on timed quizzes (a 10-minute quiz becomes 15 with 50%; the exam-mode server clock too). API tried on the Worker: every rule above, and who may do what (students and TAs can't set extra time, students can't give extensions or reopen, students not in the course refused, past dates refused). Sample mode: the new actions say they aren't saved. |

| Fix 16 Oct | The Cloudflare build, CPU limit errors on missing files, a link loop | **Done** | **Build:** the deploy build (main, after PR #61) ran 29 minutes and failed; Cloudflare's build machine has 8 GB, 2 CPUs and 20 minutes. Locally the webpack build was killed for memory. `next.config.ts` now runs webpack in its own process (`webpackBuildWorker`, not on by default with a custom webpack config), with `webpackMemoryOptimizations` and no pre-render source maps, and doesn't type-check again (`pnpm typecheck` runs before every commit); `package.json` gives it 6 GB. Built locally on 2 CPUs: 5 min 40 s, peak memory 6.9 GB for the whole machine (the webpack process about 5.5 GB). **Worth turning on (owner):** Workers & Pages → universe-web → Settings → Build → Build cache, so later builds reuse Next.js's compile cache. **Worker:** a request for a `/_next/static/` file the deployed version doesn't have (an open tab or the installed app asking for an earlier version's scripts) started the whole Next.js server and failed with "Worker exceeded CPU time limit" (88 errors in two minutes on 16 Oct); `cloudflare/worker.ts` now answers 404 at once (7 ms locally); files that exist are served before the Worker as before. **Links:** the teacher's Live class page asked for /boards about 20 times a second (a prefetching `next/link`), which tripped the tab's request brake (`src/lib/request-guard.ts`) and would have used up requests; 11 files now use `@/components/ui/Link` (no prefetching), as the house rule says. |

Booking a time (with a topic) makes a **video call in a one-to-one chat**, so it shows in both people's **Calls** with the usual 15-minute reminder, puts it in **both calendars** (batch `mentor:<booking>`) and tells the mentor; either side can cancel (the call and calendar entries go, the time is offered again). Up to 3 upcoming bookings a student. Two people booking the same time at once: exactly one gets it (the time is claimed with one conditional update; D1 has no transactions). **Be a mentor** (students and alumni on that page, teachers on Volunteer & Mentor): a profile (what you do, where, skills, bio, alumni + year, taking bookings on/off) and times (15–60 min, once or weekly up to 8 weeks, within 60 days, no overlaps, up to 40 ahead). **Job alerts**: a new opening (or one opened later) whose skills are at least half covered by a student's checked skills (`skillMatcher`) notifies them (bell + push, type `job`, in the School group), **once per opening** (`internships.alertedAt`), not if they applied, switch on Skills match (on by default). Openings now take **skills** (the admin form's new "Skills it needs" field; `skills` was silently dropped before, so skills match had nothing to match against for new openings). **Fixed on the way:** the Career page made up an "apply by" date (30 days from today) for openings without a deadline (now none is shown); the mentor list sent every mentor's **email** to anyone signed in (removed); the teacher's Volunteer & Mentor page showed a made-up "45 Verified Partners", a "Request Match" that booked a fake time (today, "10:00 AM") and an email button (now real counts, a Message button that opens a chat, and the teacher's own mentoring). **Migration 0158** (`job_applications`, `interview_answers`, `mentor_slots`; `mentor_profiles.isAlumni` / `gradYear` / `bio`; `mentorship_bookings.slotId` / `scheduledCallId`; `users.jobAlerts`; `internships.alertedAt`). `src/server/careers.ts`, `src/lib/careers.ts`, `src/components/careers/MentorSetup.tsx`; routes `/api/careers/applications`, `/interview`, `/mentors`, `/mentoring`, `/alerts`. AI use kind `interview`. Sample mode answers all five (and the teacher's mentor list), with a sample tracker and mentors. Help: `application-tracker`, `interview-practice`, `mentors`, `mentor-teacher`. 9 unit tests (`src/lib/careers.test.ts`) + 2 help tests; the cron's cheap-check test now expects 12 checks. **Browser test** (19 Oct, `scripts/checks/careers-ui.mjs` on the built Worker, 390 / 1280 px, light / dark): added an application with a link (http made https) and a document, Coming up shows it, stage moved to Interview, the cron sent "Phone screen: … at Test Co" once, edited notes, removed it; interview questions without AI (6), an answer gave 4 fillers (um ×2, like, you know), all four STAR parts and the length note, kept in history, removed; Alice booked the teacher's time from the page: a 30-minute call in their chat, 2 calendar entries, the teacher told and the call in their Calls; cancelled: call and entries gone, time free again; Alice became an alumni mentor (2025) and added a time, Bob sees her; the job alerts switch; the teacher's page has no made-up numbers or email links; sample mode shows the sample tracker, mentors and questions. A **Graphics** button in each 3D game's bar: Smooth, Balanced (the default) or Sharp, kept on the device. The automatic quality now aims for 60 frames a second (it waited for under 45), keeps what works for the next round, and no longer rebuilds every shader mid-game (~40 rebuilds, the stutters). Each round gives its graphics memory back when it ends, and the moving background behind a game rests while you play. Other problems: no game errors on the live site; the one real recent error was already fixed on 1 Nov. |

| M120 | CPU time (the owner, 6 Nov: "why the cpu time usage is till so high … only I am using this website … please fix this") | **Done** (branch `claude/youthful-fermi-sbdtcp`) | Most of the CPU went on page changes inside the app: each started the whole Next.js server on a fresh Cloudflare instance (about a second of CPU), and with one person using the site most instances were fresh. Prerendered pages' page-change data now comes from Cloudflare's cache like the pages themselves: a repeat page change on a fresh instance 1.8 s → 0.03 s of CPU (measured locally), warm ~0.1 s → 0.008 s. The spending check reads Cloudflare's numbers hourly instead of every 15 minutes. At 500 people instances stay warm, so the cost doesn't multiply by 500; CPU past the included 30 M costs $0.02 per extra million ms, but the spending guard pauses the site at 27 M (the owner's call). |

| M332 | The wheel's glide with Lenis's own clock: no slow start (the owner, 19 Nov: "it goes slow for 1 sec then it goes fast") | **Done** | M326's curve was tripist's (Lenis, intensity 25 = 2.5 s expo-out, checked in its code), but its clock restarted at each wheel event, which the browser handles after the frame's time is taken: a trackpad or a fast wheel (an event nearly every frame) stalled the page, then it rushed. Now it moves by the time since the last frame, as Lenis does; measured against the real Lenis on the landing: the same to a few pixels for a notch, a flick and a swipe. |

Not for touch, a pen or a key, not inside the app, not with Reduce Motion. |
| M334 | Sign in and sign up follow light mode (the owner, 19 Nov: "in white mode why this area is dark?") | **Done** | The sign-in frame was dark-only (`.dark` and a fixed #0b0b0c, from before light became the default in M220). Every MapLibre map shares `SMOOTH_MAP` and a longer, softer fling (`src/lib/map-smooth.ts`); no card light over maps; Trace's canvas map glides on longer. `scripts/checks/smooth-frames.mjs` measures it. |

Checked on a phone and a computer. |
| M340 | Messages: quick replies, Notes to self, shared checklists, "send when their quiet hours end", quick tools (the owner, 19 Nov: "All of them and add more quick tools") | **Done** | One-tap replies under the last message from someone else (`src/lib/quick-replies.ts`, plain rules, free); Notes to self (a chat with only you, made on first open, kept near the top: `POST /api/chat/conversations { self: true }`); checklists (`CHECKLIST` messages, `src/lib/chat-checklist.ts`, ticked by anyone in the chat through `POST /api/chat/messages/:id/check`, each tick with who did it); a bar when a 1:1 chat's other person is in quiet hours, with "Send then" scheduling the next text for when they end (`quietEnds`); `/checklist`, `/note`, `/roll`, `/flip`, `/time`. | M341 | Encrypted photos, files, videos and voice notes in private chats (the owner, 19 Nov: "encrypted message system for security") | **Done** | Private chats were end-to-end encrypted but text only. Now each file is encrypted on the sender's device with its own AES-GCM key (`src/lib/e2e-file.ts`), uploaded as scrambled `.enc` bytes (always a download), and its key, address, name and type travel inside the encrypted message; the chat's devices open it (`PrivateAttachment`). Tested with two people: the photo, a file and text open on the other side; the server holds only `application/octet-stream`. Privacy Policy and Security page mention it. |

| M401 | Hyperion: Time it, time inside functions and the run before | **Done** | After Time it: a table of the functions that used the time (how often each was called, its own time and with what it calls), and a second timed run says how much faster or slower it is than the one before, with each function's time before and after. |
| M402 | Hyperion: Sweep (a slider's whole range as a film and a table) | **Done** | Beside the sliders, Sweep runs the script once for each step of a slider (6 to 48 runs, from and to as you like): each run's figure becomes a frame you can play like a film or save as a GIF, and every number the runs leave goes in a table with a small trend line, marking where each result is largest and smallest and where it changes sign ("x is largest at theta = 42"); a press on Use puts the slider on that value. |
| M403 | Languages: Hindi kept, the other Indian languages and Urdu removed | **Done** | The language menu (and the chat translation lists) no longer offer Urdu, Bengali, Tamil, Telugu or Marathi; Hindi and the others stay. Anyone who had picked one of them sees the site in English again; a message someone writes in one of them is still named and can still be translated into a language that is offered. |
| M404 | Hyperion Simulate: Structures (stress and bending, like engineering software) | **Done** | Hyperion's menu has Simulate: choose a part (a beam, a plate with a hole, an L-bracket, a lifting lug) and its sizes, its material (8, from steel to nylon), hold it (fixed or sliding edges) and load it (a force, a pressure, a bolt pulling in a hole, its own weight); it is meshed and solved at once, as Ansys does it (finite elements), and shows the bent shape and the stress in coloured bands with the largest and smallest flagged, what the pointer is over, a plain verdict ("It holds… safety factor 1.66"), what each support carries, and a picture or the numbers to download. Checked against textbook answers. |
| M405 | Hyperion on computers only | **Done** | Hyperion no longer shows on phones and tablets: it is left out of the app switcher, the hub, the landing page and the footer there, and its pages on a phone say it is made for computers, with its link to copy or send to yourself. On a computer nothing changes. Its phone layouts were removed. |
| M406 | Hyperion Simulate: Vibration (natural frequencies) | **Done** | Beside Stress and bending, Simulate's Solution has Vibration: the part's six lowest natural frequencies (what Ansys calls a modal analysis), each shown swinging on the part, with how much of its mass moves sideways or up and down, what motor speed would set it off, and the musical note it rings at, with a button to hear it. A steel beam's first frequency comes out within 1% of the textbook formula. |
| M407 | Hyperion Simulate: Heat | **Done** | Simulate now has two kinds, Structures and Heat. Heat shows how warmth spreads through a part: edges held hot or cold, heat flowing in, air cooling its edges or faces, heat made inside it; where it settles in the end, or how it warms up over time (play it like a film), in colour, with the watts leaving through each hot or cold edge. Checked against the textbook: a bar between two temperatures, a cooling fin, an evenly heated part. |
| M408 | Hyperion Simulate: Fluids (a wind tunnel) | **Done** | Simulate's third kind: a live wind tunnel. Choose a cylinder, a wing (its thickness, curve and angle), a square or a plate and how fast and sticky the flow is (the Reynolds number); watch the flow's speed, pressure or swirl in colour with tracer particles riding it, the swirls shedding behind (a vortex street), and the drag and lift as they settle, with a live chart and how often the wake sways (Strouhal 0.20 for a cylinder, as in the books). |
| M409 | Hyperion installs as its own computer app | **Done** | Hyperion's pages now have "Install Hyperion": with Chrome or Edge it installs as a program with its own Σ icon (on the desktop, dock or start menu, with Code and Simulate as shortcuts) that opens in its own window straight into Hyperion; Safari users are told File → Add to Dock, Firefox users which browsers can. The button hides inside the installed app and on phones. |
| M410 | Hyperion Simulate: Circuits | **Done** | Simulate's fourth kind: a circuit board. Pick resistors, capacitors, coils, batteries and AC or square-wave sources, current sources and diodes, drag them between dots, join them with wires, add a ground and probes; it runs as you build, wires coloured by their voltage, each part's voltage and current when chosen, and an oscilloscope of the probes. Four circuits to start from (a capacitor charging, ringing, a rectifier, a divider). Checked against the textbook: a divider, RC charging, RLC ringing, a diode's 0.7 V, a filter's corner frequency. |
| M411 | Hyperion Simulate: draw your own part shape | **Done** | In Structures and Heat, Geometry → Shape has "Your own shape (draw it)": it starts from the shape you had (its supports and loads stay on the same sides), and you drag its corners on a grid, press a side to add a corner, nudge one with the arrow keys or remove it, add round holes and move them, or type every corner's and hole's numbers; the part is meshed and solved again as you draw, and an outline that crosses itself is pointed out. |
| M412 | Hyperion's computer app (Windows, Mac and Linux installers) | **Done** | `desktop/hyperion`: Hyperion in a window of its own (its Σ icon, File → Code and Simulate, an offline page, other sites in the normal browser, sign-in on Hyperion's tab), always the newest Hyperion. GitHub builds the installers (.exe, .dmg, .AppImage) when the owner runs "Hyperion desktop" and can publish them as the public download; signed automatically once the owner adds the certificates. Also: the sign-in page and the landing still said 13 languages (8 since M403): fixed, with a test. |
| M413 | Hyperion Simulate: 3D parts | **Done** | Structures → Geometry → "Model it as": Flat (2D) or Solid (3D). As a solid, the part (any shape, drawn ones too) gets its real thickness, is meshed with 3D elements and solved in full 3D; it is shown in 3D in its stress colours, turned with the mouse, bent and animated, with the same verdict, support forces and downloads. A solid beam bends within 3% of beam theory, and solids agree with the flat model within about 1%. |
| M414 | Hyperion toolbox: control systems | **Done** | Transfer functions are now values in Hyperion's language: `s = tf('s')`, then add, multiply and close loops (`feedback`), PID controllers (`pid`), state space (`ss`), poles, zeros, gains; and the classic tools: step and impulse responses, `stepinfo` (rise, settling, overshoot), Bode plots with the stability margins, Nyquist, root locus, `lsim`. A control.m example has sliders for Kp, Ki and Kd, so the PID can be tuned by dragging. Checked against the textbook (overshoot, peak time, −3 dB and −45°, a gain margin of 6). |
| M415 | Hyperion Simulate: Blocks (block diagrams) | **Done** | A fifth kind in Simulate: build a model out of blocks (step, sine, pulse, ramp, constant, gain, sum, product, saturation, integrator, transfer function, PID controller, scope), wire them by dragging from a block's output dot to another's input (or choose each input in the panel), and the model runs as you build it; every Scope draws what's wired into it. Three ready models: cruise control with a PI controller, a mass on a spring, a first-order loop. A loop with nothing to hold a value (an "algebraic loop") and a model that blows up are explained in plain words. Checked against exact answers (a spring's damped swing, 1 − e^(−2t), a second-order overshoot). |
| M416 | Hyperion toolbox: machine learning | **Done** | Hyperion's language learns from data: classifiers (nearest neighbours, decision trees, naive Bayes, discriminant analysis, support vector machines for two classes or more), regression (linear, logistic, regression trees), testing them fairly (cross-validation, held-out rows, confusion matrices and charts, ROC curves), clustering (k-means, DBSCAN) and principal components, from a matrix or a table. `load fisheriris` gives Fisher's 150 iris flowers to try it on; a learn.m example shows it all with a slider for the number of neighbours. `rng(seed)` now really repeats random numbers. Checked against known answers (Fisher's discriminant gets 3 flowers wrong, iris's best k-means spread 78.85, PCA's 92.46% first component, exact logistic odds, the widest SVM gap). |
| M417 | Hyperion toolbox: neural networks | **Done** | `fitcnet` (classes) and `fitrnet` (numbers): networks with as many hidden layers as wanted (relu, tanh, sigmoid or none), trained by L-BFGS, with their weights, a training history and why training stopped; they predict, cross-validate and tune like the other models. Checked: backpropagation against measured slopes, XOR learnt, iris with at most 2 mistakes, a sine wave within 0.02. |
| M418 | Hyperion toolbox: ensembles | **Done** | Many trees voting together: `fitcensemble` (bagging and random forests, AdaBoost for two classes, multi-class boosting), `fitrensemble` (least-squares boosting, bagging), `TreeBagger` (a random forest with its out-of-bag error as trees are added), `templateTree`, `oobLoss`, and `predictorImportance` for trees and ensembles. Checked: iris out-of-bag error under 8% with the petals rated most important, boosting beating a lone stump on rings, a sine wave fitted by 200 small trees. |
| M419 | Hyperion toolbox: automatic tuning | **Done** | Every model can tune its own settings: `fitcsvm(X, Y, 'OptimizeHyperparameters', 'auto')` tries settings by their cross-validated error (Bayesian optimisation by default, or a grid, or random tries), prints each try, draws the best so far and keeps the table of tries; `bayesopt` with `optimizableVariable` tunes any function. Checked: a two-setting minimum found in 25 tries (one on a log scale), iris nearest neighbours tuned to under 7% error, a grid of 5, a function's minimum within 0.2. |
| M420 | Hyperion toolbox: tree-style clustering | **Done** | Groups inside groups: `pdist` (nine kinds of distance), `squareform`, `linkage` (single, complete, average, weighted, centroid, median, Ward), `cluster` (k groups, or cut at a height or an inconsistency), `clusterdata`, `cophenet`, `inconsistent`, and `dendrogram` (the tree drawn, at most 30 leaves, the groups below 70% of the top in colours). Checked against hand-worked joins for every method, iris by Ward's method keeping the setosa flowers together. |
| M421 | Hyperion Learn: the learner window | **Done** | A new Learn page in Hyperion (beside Code and Simulate): pick data (the iris flowers, made-up shapes like two moons, circles, blobs, spirals and XOR with rows, noise and a seed, or your own CSV with its answer column and predictors), tick the models to compare (nearest neighbours, tree, naive Bayes, discriminant, SVM, neural network, random forest, boosted trees, logistic regression) and 5- or 10-fold cross-validation; each model's honest accuracy and time appear as soon as it's trained (the best marked), the chosen model's decision regions are drawn over the points with its mistakes ringed, its confusion matrix below, and Open in Code writes the same as a script. Everything trains in background workers: the page stayed at 60 frames a second while training. |
| M422 | Hyperion toolbox: optimisation | **Done** | Finding the best design: `fminsearch` (no slopes needed), `fminunc`, `fmincon` (limits of every kind: linear, equal, bounds and curved), `linprog` and `intlinprog` (whole-number answers), `quadprog`, `lsqnonlin`, `lsqcurvefit`, `fsolve`, `ga` (a genetic algorithm), `particleswarm`, with `optimset` / `optimoptions` (Display 'iter' prints each step, @optimplotfval draws the objective) and every solver's path kept so it can be drawn; an optim.m example draws three solvers' paths down Rosenbrock's valley. ### Hyperion toolboxes (the owner, 22 Nov: "need to build all of these in the downloaded app… work on this seriously")

The owner's list is MathWorks' range (applications, industries, academic disciplines, product capabilities); built as
Hyperion's own toolboxes (our code, our names; nothing copied), each a working tool in the computer app with script
functions where they belong, tested against textbook answers. Done before the list: Code (the language, plots,
symbolic maths, signals and statistics basics, files), Simulate (structures, vibration, heat, fluids, circuits). Order:
1. **3D parts** (M413) for Simulate.
2. **Control systems and model-based design**: transfer functions and state space (`tf`, `ss`, `step`, `impulse`,
   `bode`, `nyquist`, `rlocus`, `margin`, `feedback`), a PID tuner, and a block-diagram simulator (blocks, wires,
   scopes) covering physical modelling, real-time simulation and testing, variant design.
3. **AI and data**: machine learning (classification and regression learners, clustering), neural networks (train,
   test, export), predictive maintenance (vibration features, trends, remaining useful life), statistics and
   optimisation (`fmincon`-style solvers with the path drawn), embedded AI (run a trained model in a script).
4. **Electrical and energy**: power electronics (switches, MOSFETs and PWM in Circuits; buck and boost converters),
   batteries (cell models, state of charge with a Kalman filter, packs, a battery management system), power systems
   (load flow, faults, a small grid), mixed-signal (ADC, DAC, PLL), energy production (solar, wind).
5. **Signals, comms and sensing**: a signal analyser and filter designer, wireless communications (modulation,
   constellations, channels, bit-error curves), radar (range, Doppler, pulse compression), image processing and
   computer vision (filters, edges, segmentation, the camera), test and measurement (the microphone, serial devices,
   data logging), the Internet of Things (devices sending data, dashboards).
6. **Machines and vehicles**: robotics and mechatronics (arm kinematics and dynamics, path planning), automated driving
   and vehicles (vehicle dynamics, lane keeping, electric drive), aerospace (flight dynamics, orbits), railways.
7. **Sciences and industries**: biology and biotech (epidemics, pharmacokinetics, DNA tools), chemistry and chemical
   engineering (kinetics, equilibria, reactors), neuroscience (neuron and network models), physics (mechanics, waves,
   fields), geoscience (seismic and map data), medical devices (ECG and biosignals), finance (options, portfolios,
   risk), mathematics teaching.
8. **Capabilities**: C code from scripts (embedded code generation), parallel computing (workers, `parfor`), GPU
   computing (WebGPU), discrete-event simulation, unit tests and checks for scripts (verification), hardware support
   (Arduino and serial), reports (done in M397), deployment (share links M398, the computer app M412).

### Ghidra, the one AI (the owner, 23 Nov: "we need only ghidra as one main ai and i need it to do all the work in this project")

People meet one AI, Ghidra, everywhere. The app agents (Support, Travel, Social, Research), Hyperion's AI, the tutor and
the ~45 other AI features become Ghidra's **skills**, chosen from the page and the question; no other AI names in what
people see. Every answer goes through one brain: UniVerse's own add-on on Cloudflare where it scores higher, else Gemini
(DeepSeek for hard maths and code, behind its switch), Ghidra 2 on the device when offline. Big asks are split into
steps confirmed together (the chaining step, `ghidra-chain.ts`: the multi-agent system, invisible). One set of
guardrails (`ghidra-guard.ts`); Ghidra sees and does only what the person may; actions wait for their tap.

1. **Skills and the brain** (free, M443 done; the jobs' instruction texts move into the list with step 2): one list (`src/server/ghidra/skills.ts`), one brain (`src/server/ghidra/brain.ts`)
   that every AI call goes through (nothing else imports `gemini.ts`, a test fails a break); the agents' and Hyperion's
   own-model paths inside it; the old AI names in screens become Ghidra. Done when every AI feature answers through it.
2. **One exam** (free; M444 built): 50–150 questions per skill with pass / fail rules, one command prints Ghidra's score card
   (the agents' rules first; loosen support's `nums` for numbered steps). Done when every skill has a starting score:
   built (`src/server/ghidra/exam`, `scripts/ai/ghidra-exam.mjs`). Starting scores: our own model 80% (M445); the live
   Gemini brain's comes from the owner's first console run (Console → Health → Ghidra's exam).
3. **👍 / 👎 on every answer** (free): "What was wrong?", "Better answer"; no names, emails or phone numbers; kept 90
   days; each person deletes theirs in Settings; each skill's rating in the console. Done when ratings come in.
4. **Ghidra's add-on** (paid, ~US$10–20, [owner] yes first): a stronger open teacher (Qwen2.5-72B) writes lessons per
   skill, kept only if they pass the rules, plus people's corrected answers; one add-on `GHIDRA_LORA` replaces
   `AGENTS_LORA` and `HYPERION_LORA`; switched on per skill only where it scores higher. Results on a RunPod network
   volume. No Gemini answers as lessons (Google's terms).
5. **Ghidra offline** (paid, ~US$3): Ghidra 2 trained on the card with the same lessons, as the offline backup; the old
   letter Ghidra removed.
6. **Every month** (~US$5–15): new ratings, train, score, switch only on a win. The paid-AI daily limit (M442) stays.

Later: web search stays behind its switch (off). Check the name "Ghidra" (also the NSA's free tool) before advertising
it widely or putting it in the app stores.
